the rest it recorded nav + at most one table, never columns (except Cost Explorer /
Budgets / CloudTrail) and never detail tabs or wizards.

## 17. Top gaps (ordered by exam/learning impact)

1. **CloudWatch has no console structure at all** — no left nav, no Home, no
   Overview, no Dashboards, no Logs (Log groups / Insights / Live tail /
   Contributor Insights), no Events, no X-Ray, no graphs, no date-range or
   dashboard controls. 18 of 20 survey nav items missing; the page is three
   stacked cards (component health, alarms, metrics). Worst gap in Group C.
   The backup file's claim of a Logs/Dashboards nav, graphs, and wizard is false
   (§14c) — do not build from it without re-checking.
2. **Cost Explorer is a breakdown table, not the Billing console** — 13 of 17
   Billing nav items missing (Cost analysis, Cost and usage report + export,
   Purchase recommendations, Reservation utilization, anomaly detection, cost
   allocation/tags, Reports, Credits, Billing preferences, Billing); no chart,
   no summary cards beyond a total in a title, no Report parameters, no Save
   report. Plus the registry/console fidelity mismatch (PARTIAL vs SIMULATED).
3. **CloudTrail is Event history only** — 8 of 12 nav items missing (Lake, Data
   events, Event data stores, Event selectors, Channels, Queries, Dashboards,
   Settings); Trails/Insights are disabled cards; the event table matches 0/3
   survey columns (Time/Actor/Action/Resource type/Resource vs Event name/Event
   time/Event source) because it is CloudForge's own audit log, not CloudTrail
   events; no Create trail / Start logging.
4. **WAF is Web ACLs only** — 9 of 12 nav items missing (Bot Control, IP sets,
   Regex pattern sets, AI traffic monetization, all Shield items, Shield network
   security director, Firewall Manager); Rules/Rule groups are home cards, not
   sections; an ACL can hold exactly one rule, created inline — no rule
   management, priorities, or logging.
5. **Literal JavaScript source on 7 Home dashboards** — Inspector, Config, WAF,
   KMS, Secrets Manager, Trusted Advisor, Organizations render
   `"x.isError ? '…' : \`${n} …\`"` as visible Health text (cross-cutting
   defect). One-line fix per console (backticks), high visibility, zero backend
   work. Should be fixed before any v4 UI claim.
6. **KMS is 1 of 5 nav items** — AWS managed keys, Keys shared with me, Custom
   key stores, Settings all missing; the Aliases card promises "Aliases on the
   key detail" where no alias feature exists; no key-policy tab (policy survey:
   not observable, so SDNR — but the gap is real if v4 builds key detail).
7. **SSM is a single page, not Systems Manager** — no nav, no Home; vs the backup
   (PUB) layout: Session Manager, Patch Manager, State Manager, Documents,
   Parameter Store, Automation, OpsCenter/Explorer all missing; Fleet Manager and
   Run Command are reduced tables; Run Command has no document picker or wizard.
   Parameter Store is the highest-value addition (backup §4) and unblocks
   Lambda/ECS/CodeBuild patterns.
8. **No detail tabs, no wizards, no search/pagination/Preferences anywhere in
   Group C.** Every console is a two-item nav (or none) + inline forms. This is
   the single largest structural difference from AWS across all 14 services, and
   it cannot be verified against the survey (which recorded no detail tabs or
   wizard steps) — it is a design gap, not a survey mismatch, and should be
   stated as such in v4 rather than claimed as matching.
9. **Restricted services cannot be called matches.** GuardDuty, Security Hub,
   Organizations (and DMS, outside this group) were RESTRICTED in the survey;
   Inspector, Config, Trusted Advisor were never recorded. Their CloudForge
   layouts follow public docs (GuardDuty/Security Hub/Organizations say so in
   their banners; Inspector/Config/Trusted Advisor do not even say that).
   Their counted items are 84 SDNR + 6 invented Home dashboards — honest, but unvalidated.
10. **Budgets is the closest to matching and still misses the chrome** — 7/8
    columns + tab match, but no Reports tab, no checkbox, no "Find a budget"
    search, no pagination, no Preferences gear, and the Thresholds column shows
    invented ALERT text instead of threshold values.

## 18. What is genuinely good (for the v4 report's balance)

- Budgets routing fix from v3.2 verified present; shared table component means
  Cost Explorer and `/services/budgets` cannot drift (§10a).
- Cost Explorer breakdown 3/3 columns match, grouped from real priced usage
  (test-asserted); Budgets 6/7 content columns match with honest "Not available"
  for forecasts.
- Honest labelling is consistent: WAF "does not block live traffic", KMS "NOT
  real AWS KMS, no HSM", Secrets "no auto-rotation", CloudTrail "CloudForge's
  own log, not AWS CloudTrail", GuardDuty/Security Hub/Organizations banners
  state the RESTRICTED survey outcome, SSM/Inspector/Config state real-evidence
  rules and 503 behaviour. No invented counts anywhere in Group C (all
  zero-count cards are disabled with reasons).
- Real backends behind the tables that exist: KMS envelope encryption with
  rotation, Secrets envelope-encrypted + versioned, WAF real rule evaluation,
  GuardDuty findings only from real audit evidence, Config rules evaluated
  against real resources, CloudTrail real audit store with export jobs,
  CloudWatch real Prometheus series + live `/readyz`.

## 19. Limitations of this audit

- Read-only code inspection; no build, no test run, no live browser, no running
  stack. Behavioural claims (e.g. "rotate keeps previous versions decryptable",
  "re-scans dedupe") are taken from code comments and mutation handlers, not
  executed.
- No git history available in this checkout (`~/workspace/cloudforge` is not a
  git repository), so "changed since v3.2" could not be diffed; v3.2 claims were
  re-checked against current file contents only.
- AWS details are taken only from the two survey files and the two backup files
  named at the top. Anything AWS does that no survey file records is SDNR here,
  even when it is well-known AWS behaviour (e.g. KMS wizard steps, Secrets
  rotation config, WAF rule priorities).
- Line numbers refer to the files as read on 2026-11-22; they will shift with
  any edit.


## Part D — Group D — remaining lite services + cross-cutting (Aurora, Backup, Macie, Audit Manager, Artifact, CloudFront, EFS, FSx, Shield, Control Tower, Identity Center, RAM, Direct Connect, Global Accelerator, MGN, Compute Optimizer, Savings Plans, DMS, DataSync, VPC Endpoints, Network Firewall, Flow Logs, Transit Gateway, VPN, EBS, Auto Scaling, VPC sections)

Counts: no survey-recorded list column missing anywhere in Group D (84 columns: 74 matching, 10 partial, 0 missing); 17 of 31 services unverifiable by design (survey records nothing)

# CloudForge v4 audit — Group D: remaining lite services + cross-cutting bugs

Date: 2026-11-22. Auditor: subagent (Group D). Scope: 31 services —
Aurora, Backup, Macie, Audit Manager, Artifact, CloudFront, EFS, FSx, Shield,
Control Tower, IAM Identity Center, RAM, Direct Connect, Global Accelerator,
Application Migration (MGN), Compute Optimizer, Savings Plans, DMS, DataSync,
VPC Endpoints, Network Firewall, VPC Flow Logs, Transit Gateway, VPN, EBS,
Auto Scaling, Subnets, Route Tables, Internet Gateway, NAT Gateway,
Security Groups.

## Method and sources

- Baselines read first: `docs/reference/aws-console-survey-2026-11-22.md`
  (46/46 services, ap-southeast-2, read-only; **no create/edit wizard was
  opened for any service**, so no wizard steps are recorded for any service)
  and `docs/reference/tab-audit-full-2026-11-22.md` (the v3.2 audit).
- Every claim below was re-checked against the actual current code in
  `frontend/src/pages/` (and `backend/internal/httpapi/services.go` for
  registry fidelity). The v3.2 audit was **not** copied blindly — several of
  its claims are now wrong or imprecise; corrections are flagged
  `CORRECTION vs v3.2 audit` inline.
- Verdicts per item: **matches AWS** / **partial** / **missing** /
  **invented** (CloudForge-only, not in AWS) / **survey-does-not-record**
  (cannot be verified). No AWS details were invented.
- Key survey-coverage fact that dominates this group: the survey records
  **no list columns and no detail tabs for most Group D services**, and
  records **nothing at all** for Aurora, Macie, Audit Manager, Artifact,
  FSx, Shield (standalone), Control Tower, Identity Center, RAM,
  Direct Connect, Global Accelerator, MGN, Compute Optimizer, Flow Logs,
  Subnets, Route Tables, Internet Gateway, NAT Gateway, Security Groups
  (list columns). DMS was NOT ACCESSIBLE (no layout captured). So for
  17 of the 31 services the only verifiable AWS facts are "survey records
  nothing" — everything CloudForge shows for them is CloudForge's own
  structure, honestly labelled as such in the code.

## Shared scaffolding (applies to all 17 exam-service consoles)

All of Aurora, Backup, Macie, Audit Manager, Artifact, CloudFront, EFS,
FSx, Shield, Control Tower, Identity Center, RAM, Direct Connect,
Global Accelerator, MGN, Compute Optimizer, Savings Plans are rendered by
the shared builder `frontend/src/pages/ExamServiceConsole.tsx`
(`ExamServiceConsole`, configs in `ExamServiceConsoles.tsx`). Shared
behaviour, verified in the builder (not per-service):

- Left nav (subNav) is always: `<Name> dashboard` (Home), the list tab,
  any `extraTabs`, `Operations`, `Learning`. There is **no per-service
  left-nav grouping beyond `group` on extraTabs** (Backup is the only
  config that uses groups: "My account" / "External resources" /
  "My organization" / "Backup Audit Manager").
- Home dashboard is the VPC-pattern `ServiceHomeDashboard`: banner,
  action links (Create + Learning Center), "Resources by Region" cards
  from the **live list query** (counts are real), Service Health bullets,
  Settings (Resource Explorer / All services links), Additional
  Information (Learning Center / certification map), plus a
  "What you can do instead" card (from the still-present
  `LITE_GENERIC_SERVICES` map) and any `homeExtra`. Exception: Backup
  Vaults/Jobs home cards show count `—` with a note to open the tab —
  not live counts (minor inconsistency with the other cards).
- List page: **no search box, no filter, no checkbox column, no
  pagination** on any exam-service list. `AwsTable`
  (`frontend/src/components/AwsTable.tsx`) only renders its filter input
  when `onFilterChange` is passed — `ExamServiceConsole` never passes it,
  never passes `selectable`. Toolbar is only: count text
  (`N <kind>(s) · API-emulated records — nothing here provisions real AWS
  infrastructure.`), optional `listActions`, Refresh button, Preferences
  button (always disabled: "Table preferences are not persisted in
  CloudForge."). AwsTable's own docstring claims a "pagination footer"
  but the code has only a count line — **no pagination exists anywhere
  in AwsTable**.
- List columns always end with an extra `Actions` column (Open / Delete
  text buttons) — invented, not survey-recorded, on every exam console.
- Create is a **single inline card, not a multi-step wizard**: Name field
  + the config's `createFields` (text / select / number / list).
  Validation is only "Name non-empty" (submit button disabled while the
  name is blank). Number fields have no frontend range enforcement
  (e.g. Aurora instance count hint "1–3", Savings Plans commitment hint
  "$0–100 per hour" — hints only). Selects offer a blank "— select —"
  option even when the field is `required: true`. Every create card
  carries the footnote: "Create form is CloudForge's own — the
  2026-11-22 survey opened no create wizard, so no AWS wizard steps
  were recorded for this service." — correct; **all wizard steps are
  survey-does-not-record for every service in this group**.
- Detail on a selected row: an auto-rendered `Details` tab (every stored
  record field as a definition list, ID/Name first) plus the config's
  own `detailTabs`. There is **no row-click-to-open**; opening is via the
  `Actions → Open` link. Delete is available both per-row and in the
  detail header. Detail-tab names/order are CloudForge's own for every
  service — **the survey records detail tabs for no Group D service**,
  so none of them can be verified as a match.
- Operations tab: registry operations table (Operation / Fidelity /
  Supported / HTTP route / Limitations) + Details/Limitations tabs on a
  selected operation; the "Run <operation>" button is **always
  disabled** (reason shown: no generic runner, or no HTTP route).
  Learning tab: links to Learning Center tracks + the generic note.
- Error states: list load failure → `ErrorBox "<List> could not be

  honest limit in plain language.

  Extra card: "Backup scripts (host-side)" — `scripts/backup.sh` /
  `scripts/restore.sh`, 7-day retention, restore destructive without
  `--force`, no restore API. Invented but honest and useful.
- List columns — Backup plans (exact order): Plan, Schedule, Retention
  (days), Resources (count), Status, Created, + Actions. Vaults:
  Vault name, Status, Created, Actions. Jobs: Job, Plan, Status, Size,

  (detail tab): Job, Status, Size, SHA-256, Started, Completed, Error.

  validation in the frontend. Vault create: Name only.
- Detail tabs: Details (auto) + Run history ("Run plan now (real
  pg_dump)" button + jobs-for-this-plan table; note that a run is a
  real `pg_dump -Fc` with SHA-256, FAILED with the reason when no DB

  Preferences (shared). Nothing else disabled.
- `CORRECTION vs v3.2 audit §3c`: Backup is **no longer generic** — it
  has a dedicated console with a real backend (plans/vaults/jobs).

## 32. Cross-cutting (1): the literal template-string bug — DataSync is NOT affected in the current tree

**Task premise correction.** The task states that in
`frontend/src/pages/MigrationConsoles.tsx` the DataSync Service Health
bullet renders the literal string
`tasks.isError ? 'Tasks could not be loaded.' : \`${taskRows.length} task(s) listed.\``.
Re-checked against the current tree: **that is not present**.
`MigrationConsoles.tsx:106` (DMS) and `:358` (DataSync) are both correct
unquoted ternaries:

```tsx
tasks.isError ? 'Tasks could not be loaded.' : `${taskRows.length} task(s) listed.`,
```

The same is true of `ExamServiceConsole.tsx:215`, `Ec2Console.tsx:271`,
`ElbConsole.tsx:294`, `LambdaConsole.tsx:183`,
`NetworkingConsoles.tsx:691` (Transit Gateway shell) and the
SecurityConsoles DMS-adjacent consoles at `:116`/`:207`. So the live
host's DataSync literal (seen 2026-11-22) must come from an **older
deployed build** than this tree — or the report misattributed a
different console's bullet. Either way, DataSync/DMS do not have this
bug in the current code, and no fix is needed there.

**The bug is real and widespread elsewhere: 20 literal-string
occurrences in `frontend/src`.** Each is a double-quoted string
containing a ternary with backticks, placed in a `health={[...]}` array
that `ServiceHomeDashboard` renders verbatim
(`ServiceHome.tsx:162-170`: `health.map((h) => <li key={h}>{h}</li>)` —
no evaluation). The user therefore sees the source text, e.g.
``endpoints.isError ? 'Endpoints could not be loaded.' : `3 endpoint(s) listed.` ``,
instead of a count. Full list (file:line, variable):

Group D (3):
1. `frontend/src/pages/NetworkingConsoles.tsx:281` — VPC Endpoints
   (`endpoints.isError … ${rows.length} endpoint(s) listed.`)
2. `frontend/src/pages/NetworkingConsoles.tsx:420` — Network Firewall
   (`firewalls.isError … ${rows.length} firewall(s) listed.`)
3. `frontend/src/pages/NetworkingConsoles.tsx:586` — VPC Flow Logs
   (`groups.isError … ${rows.length} group(s) listed.`)

Outside Group D (17) — same bug, same fix (drop the surrounding
double quotes so the ternary evaluates):
4. `frontend/src/pages/RdsConsole.tsx:115` — `instances.isError …`
5. `frontend/src/pages/ElastiCacheConsole.tsx:119` — `clusters.isError …`
6. `frontend/src/pages/Route53Console.tsx:100` — `zones.isError …`
7. `frontend/src/pages/SqsConsole.tsx:109` — `queues.isError …`
8. `frontend/src/pages/SnsConsole.tsx:111` — `topics.isError …`
9. `frontend/src/pages/EventBridgeConsole.tsx:86` — `rules.isError …`
10. `frontend/src/pages/ApiGatewayConsole.tsx:125` — `apis.isError …`
11. `frontend/src/pages/EcsConsole.tsx:202` — `clusters.isError …`
12. `frontend/src/pages/AnalyticsConsoles.tsx:337` — Kinesis streams
    (`streams.isError …`)
13. `frontend/src/pages/AnalyticsConsoles.tsx:478` — Firehose delivery
    streams (`streams.isError …`)
14. `frontend/src/pages/SecurityConsoles.tsx:296` — Inspector
    (`assessments.isError …`)
15. `frontend/src/pages/SecurityConsoles.tsx:431` — Config
    (`rules.isError …`)
16. `frontend/src/pages/SecurityConsoles.tsx:583` — WAF
    (`acls.isError …`)
17. `frontend/src/pages/SecurityConsoles.tsx:737` — KMS
    (`keys.isError …`)
18. `frontend/src/pages/SecurityConsoles.tsx:873` — Secrets Manager
    (`secrets.isError …`)
19. `frontend/src/pages/ManagementConsoles.tsx:166` — Trusted Advisor
    (`results.isError …`)
20. `frontend/src/pages/ManagementConsoles.tsx:479` — Organizations
    (`units.isError …`)

Note the pattern is easy to miss in review because the quoted string
is syntactically valid and TypeScript-clean — it type-checks as a
`string` in a `string[]`. The correct sites (DMS, DataSync, EC2, ELB,
Lambda, Transit Gateway shell, ExamServiceConsole) show the intended
form. No other literal-ternary variants were found (grep for
`"[a-zA-Z_.]+\.isError ?` across `frontend/src` returns exactly the
20 above; `ManagementConsoles.tsx:346` is a correct unquoted ternary).

## 33. Cross-cutting (2): stale version string `0.9.0-m9`

Every place the string appears (grep `0\.9\.0` across repo, excluding
unrelated `0.9.x` library versions):

| Location | What it is |
|---|---|
| `backend/internal/httpapi/httpapi.go:46` | `const Version = "0.9.0-m9"` — **the source**. Comment line 45: "Version is the M9 API version string." Served at `GET /api/v1/health` (`httpapi.go:674`: `"version": Version`). |
| `frontend/src/pages/console-home.test.tsx:46,70` | Test mocks health `{version: '0.9.0-m9', …}` and asserts the page contains `0.9.0-m9`. |
| `frontend/src/pages/Labs.test.tsx:28` | Test mocks the same health payload. |
| `docs/api/reference.md:10` | "Version: `0.9.0-m9` (see `GET /api/v1/health`)." |
| `MILESTONE_LOG.md:425,444,615` | Historical log entries (M9 bump `0.8.0-m8` → `0.9.0-m9`; live-walk transcripts). Historical, not live. |
| `docs/phase4-live-walk/transcript-phase4.txt:2` | Historical transcript JSON with `"version":"0.9.0-m9"`. Historical. |

Live surface: the main Dashboard renders the live value from the
health endpoint — `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:162`
(`{health.data.version}`, parsed in `frontend/src/services/api.ts:174`).
So the stale string is **user-visible on the Dashboard** (this matches
the 2026-11-22 21:40 IST live check: "Version still reports 0.9.0-m9").
It is stale because the tree has since shipped v2, v3, v3.1 and v3.2
with no version bump: the constant still says M9. Unrelated versions
that must **not** be touched: `frontend/package.json` `version:
"0.1.0"` (the frontend package's own version, unrelated to the API
version) and `backend/internal/hostagent/ops.go:38` `const Version =
"0.3.0-m3"` (the host agent's own version). Fix (for the coordinator):
bump the `httpapi.go:46` constant (and the two test mocks + the API
reference doc) to the current release version; the Dashboard will pick
it up automatically.

## 34. Cross-cutting (3): does any Group D service still fall back to generic `LiteServiceConsole` / `ServiceDetail`?

**No — not via any canonical route.** Verified in
`frontend/src/App.tsx` and `frontend/src/pages/ServiceDetail.tsx`:

- All 17 exam services have dedicated routes (`App.tsx:135-153`):
  aurora, backup, macie, auditmanager, artifact, cloudfront, efs,
  fsx, shield, controltower, identitycenter, ram, directconnect,
  globalaccelerator, mgn, computeoptimizer, savingsplans.
- DMS, DataSync, VPC Endpoints, Network Firewall, Flow Logs have
  dedicated consoles (`App.tsx:111-112,124-126`).
- Transit Gateway, VPN, EBS, Auto Scaling redirect to VPC/EC2
  sections (`App.tsx:80` nacl→vpc, `:88` elb→ec2, `:123`
  transitgateway→vpc, `:134` autoscaling→ec2, `:141` ebs→ec2, `:148`
  vpn→vpc). Subnets/Route Tables/IGW/NAT/SG are VPC sections
  (and `ServiceDetail.tsx`'s `DEDICATED` map redirects
  subnet/routetable/igw/nat/sg → `/services/vpc`).
- `ServiceDetail` (`/services/:id` catch-all, `App.tsx:161`) only
  renders `LiteServiceConsole` when the id is in
  `LITE_GENERIC_SERVICES` **and** no dedicated route/redirect matched
  first. React Router ranks the static routes above `:id`, so for
  every Group D id the dedicated console or redirect wins.
  `LiteServiceConsole` is therefore **unreachable dead fallback code
  for all 31 Group D services** via normal routing.

Two stale-code notes that follow from this:

- `frontend/src/services/liteServices.ts` still lists 19 entries
  (the 17 exam services + autoscaling, ebs, vpn) under a header that
  says "generic services with no dedicated console and no backend
  runtime" — **that description is now false for all 19**. The map is
  still read in two live places: `ServiceDetail.tsx:43` (the dead
  fallback branch) and `ExamServiceConsole.tsx:119` (for the "What you
  can do instead" card on every exam console — that use is live and
  intentional). The map's `note` fields were updated to point at the
  dedicated consoles, but the file header was not.
- The v3.2 audit's §3c "17 generic services" verdict and §3d
  (Budgets/NACLs on ServiceDetail) are both superseded: the 17 now
  have dedicated consoles, and `/services/nacl` redirects to the VPC
  NACLs section (`App.tsx:80`), `/services/budgets` renders
  `BudgetsConsole` (`App.tsx:130`).

## 35. Registry-vs-console contradictions (found while auditing Group D)

`backend/internal/httpapi/services.go` still describes three services
as if the v3.2 backends existed, while the consoles are honest
UNSUPPORTED empty states. The registry is what the Operations tab and
the service catalogue show, so these are user-visible contradictions:

- **VPN** (`services.go:590`): registry fidelity API_EMULATED,
  description "Site-to-site VPN: customer gateways, VPN gateways and
  connections — durable records", summary "Customer gateways / VPN
  gateways / Connections; Tunnel activate/deactivate", differs "Tunnel
  state is a teaching field: activating a tunnel records it UP, but no
  IPsec tunnel exists…". Console (`VpcConsole.tsx:258`): UNSUPPORTED,
  empty 19-column table, no create, no gateways, no tunnels. **No VPN
  API exists.**
- **EBS** (`services.go:515`): registry API_EMULATED, "Block storage
  volumes — durable records with attach/detach against real labs",
  summary "Volumes list; Create-volume wizard; Attach/Detach actions",
  differs "Volumes are durable records, not real block devices…
  Attach validates that the lab exists… In-use volumes cannot be
  deleted." Console (`Ec2Console.tsx:704`): UNSUPPORTED, empty
  23-column table, no create/attach/detach. **No volume API exists.**
- **Auto Scaling** (`services.go:825`): registry API_EMULATED, "Auto
  Scaling groups — durable records with min/desired/max and scaling",
  summary "Auto Scaling groups list; Create-group wizard; Scale
  action", differs "Groups record min/desired/max… scaling sets the
  recorded desired capacity. No instances are launched or terminated…
  Group size capped at 10." Console (`Ec2Console.tsx:660`):
  UNSUPPORTED, disabled Create button, no list/wizard/scale.
  **No scaling API exists.**

(The same pattern may exist for other services outside Group D; not
checked here. The Operations tab on the exam consoles reads the
registry, so the exam services' registry entries should also be
spot-checked — their `http_route` values are generated from the real
exam backend in `services.go:358-386` and looked consistent for the
sample checked: savingsplans, aurora, cloudfront.)

## Top gaps (Group D)

1. **The literal template-string bug is live in 3 Group D consoles**
   (VPC Endpoints, Network Firewall, Flow Logs — §32) and 17 more
   places outside the group. DataSync/DMS are **not** affected in the
   current tree despite the task premise (and the live host's DataSync
   literal) — the host is evidently running an older build.
2. **Registry lies about VPN, EBS and Auto Scaling** (§35): the
   registry advertises API_EMULATED backends with create wizards and
   actions that do not exist; the consoles honestly say UNSUPPORTED.
   The registry is what the catalogue/Operations tab shows.
3. **VPN is the thinnest real section**: 19/19 columns but 4/5 nav
   items missing and 0/6 toolbar controls (no Create button at all).
   EBS is the same shape (23/23 columns, 0/7 toolbar controls, no
   Create volume button). Both are honest empty tables, but an AWS
   user reaching them finds no working entry point.
4. **DataSync's toolbar is mostly missing**: 4/4 tables have the
   survey's columns, but Actions/Edit/Stop/Delete-disabled, Find
   search, Preferences, pagination, Create location, Create agent and
   the status filter are all absent; Task executions / Locations /
   Agents are same-page sections, not nav items, and Dashboard has no
   "New" badge.
5. **17 of 31 services are unverifiable by design**: the survey records
   nothing for Aurora, Macie, Audit Manager, Artifact, FSx, Control
   Tower, Identity Center, RAM, Direct Connect, Global Accelerator,
   MGN, Compute Optimizer, Flow Logs (and no columns/tabs for the VPC
   sub-services). Their consoles are CloudForge's own structure —
   honestly labelled, but "matching AWS" cannot be claimed for any of
   them. DMS is in the same boat (NOT ACCESSIBLE in the survey).
6. **No search, filter, checkbox selection or pagination on any Group
   D list.** The survey records search/filter/Preferences/pagination
   for S3-style lists, CloudFront, DataSync, VPN and EBS; CloudForge
   has Refresh + disabled Preferences only (exam consoles), and
   nothing at all on the EBS/VPN tables. `AwsTable` has no pagination
   implementation despite its docstring.
7. **No create/edit wizard steps are survey-verified for any service**
   (the survey opened none). All CloudForge create forms are single
   inline cards with Name-only validation; number ranges and required
   selects are unenforced in the frontend. DMS has no endpoints table
   despite a home card for Endpoints; VPC Endpoints / Network Firewall
   / Flow Logs have no row detail at all (or a single panel, no tabs).
8. **Stale version `0.9.0-m9`** (§33) is still served by
   `GET /api/v1/health` and displayed on the main Dashboard, three
   releases after M9.
9. **Stale fallback code**: `LITE_GENERIC_SERVICES` still describes
   19 services as having "no dedicated console and no backend runtime"
   (false for all 19), and `LiteServiceConsole` is now unreachable dead
   code for Group D — harmless, but the next reader will be misled.
10. **Smaller items**: Backup's Vaults/Jobs home cards show `—`
    instead of live counts; CloudFront `Invalidations` appears twice
    in the nav; EFS lands on the list, not the survey's get-started
    page, and its Backup/DataSync/Transfer/Documentation links are
    missing; Auto Scaling is missing Benefits, Pricing and the
    marketing hero; Transit Gateway is missing Multicast and Metering
    policies; NAT Gateway has no Elastic IP association; the VPC
    sections share one generic 6-column table (ID/Name/CIDR/State/
    Parent/Fidelity) for five different resource types.

## Addendum — fresh v4 survey (2026-11-22, 21:57–22:20 IST, ap-southeast-2, read-only)

Primary source for this addendum: docs/reference/aws-console-survey-v4-2026-11-22.md (106 lines). It is the authority for anything it records; where it says NOT OBSERVABLE, nothing is invented. The older survey and the audit above remain the source for services the new pass did not reach (Kinesis, MSK, Redshift, Neptune, EKS, CodePipeline/CodeBuild, Glue, Athena, EMR, SES, Global Accelerator, Route 53 Resolver endpoints, Cloud WAN, Network Firewall, Verified Access, PrivateLink endpoints, Lattice).

Key changes vs the old survey and the audit above (delta files: /tmp/v4-survey-delta-1.md, /tmp/v4-survey-delta-2.md):

1. **Detail tabs observed for the first time — only VPC and IAM.** VPC (default VPC): Resource map, CIDRs, Flow logs, Tags, Related resources, Integrations. IAM role: Permissions, Trust relationships, Tags, Last Accessed, Revoke sessions. IAM policy (AdministratorAccess): Permissions, Entities attached, Policy versions (1), Last Accessed. Every other service's detail tabs are NOT OBSERVABLE (empty tables) — never invented.
2. **Wizards observed (all left via Cancel, nothing submitted):** Create VPC, Launch instances, Lambda Create Event Function (Node.js 26.x default, Durable execution / EC2 capacity provider toggles).
3. **S3 contradiction (flagged, not resolved):** old survey Buckets columns included IAM Access Analyzer; new survey shows Name / AWS Region / Creation date with NO IAM Access Analyzer column. Do not delete the column on one pass alone — it may be conditional on an analyzer existing; needs a non-empty account to resolve.
4. **WAF is a different console now (wafv2-pro):** "Protection packs (web ACLs)", 10 new columns, Rules and Bot Control gone from the nav, many new sub-items. Code still follows the old WAF console.
5. **Cost Explorer breakdown changed shape** (Service/% → Total + May–Oct 2026 monthly columns); Billing nav shrank to 3 items (Cost Explorer, Free Tier, Budgets). Code's old breakdown match no longer holds.
6. **Nav restructures:** EC2 (+UltraServers, +Capacity Manager, +AWS Global View, +Events; −Systems Manager, −Account settings, −EBS Snapshots/Public Images), Lambda (+Web Functions, +Test events, +Code signing configurations, +Settings; −Replicas), Route 53 (Resolver split, Global Resolver, IP-based routing, ARC), CloudTrail (+Data event coverage (New), Lake regrouped + Integrations), CloudWatch (new Ingestion/AI Operations/GenAI/Omni nav), EventBridge (Rules redirect to Event buses; Schedule groups, Partner event sources, API destinations, Connections, Schemas, Global endpoints, Learn, Sandbox; Event streams/Settings dropped), IAM simplified to 4 nav items (Roles, Policies, IAM users, Root access management).
7. **First-ever exact columns:** SQS (7), SNS (3), EventBridge buses (5), API Gateway (8), ECS clusters (6), Transit Gateway gateways (4), EFS (12), VPC 17 + Subnets 21, EC2 Instances 17, IAM Policies (Type, Used as) + IAM Users 9, Lambda (+Package type, +Type), DynamoDB 12, ElastiCache Valkey 6, KMS 6, Secrets 4, WAF Protection packs 10. RDS/Route 53 columns stay NOT OBSERVABLE.
8. **VPN columns grew 19 → 23:** CIDR split into 4 explicit columns + new "Core network attachment ARN".
9. **DataSync is a pure confirm:** new survey matches the old retry exactly (same 5 nav, same 29 columns). Zero delta.
10. **Restricted-dialog nav recorded for the first time:** FSx (14 items), DMS (6 nav + 4 dashboard counts), Organizations (3), Direct Connect (9) — labels behind dialogs only.
11. **Code already changed since the audit (builds in progress):** at least 7 audit findings are stale — IAM policy detail tabs now present 4/4, VPC's 4 sections present as UNSUPPORTED, WAF/KMS/CloudTrail/CloudWatch nav labels present, Budgets Reports tab + search present, IAM editor Specify/Review + Import + MFA/IP present, DataSync all 5 nav items as real sections, VPN now a real API-emulated backend. Code verdicts in the deltas are "as of read time" only.


## Delta Part 1 — core (VPC, EC2, IAM, S3, RDS, Lambda, Route 53, DynamoDB, ElastiCache, WAF, CloudTrail, KMS, Secrets, Cost Explorer/Budgets)

# CloudForge v4 — survey-delta audit, Group 1 (core)

Date: 2026-11-22 (~22:22 IST, read time).
Scope: VPC, EC2, IAM, S3, RDS, Lambda, Route 53, DynamoDB, ElastiCache,
WAF, CloudTrail, KMS, Secrets Manager, Cost Explorer/Budgets.
CloudWatch is included as a bonus because `CloudWatchConsole.tsx` was
listed for spot-check.

## Sources

- NEW primary survey (authority):
  `docs/reference/aws-console-survey-v4-2026-11-22.md` — fresh, 2026-11-22
  21:57–22:20 IST, region ap-southeast-2 (IAM/Route 53/CloudFront/WAF/
  Organizations/Billing global, us-east-1), account 867109003096
  "Ready Set Build". READ-ONLY; only wizards opened were Create VPC,
  Launch instances, Lambda Create Event Function — all left via Cancel,
  nothing submitted.
- OLD survey: `docs/reference/aws-console-survey-2026-11-22.md` (46/46
  services; no wizard opened; no detail tabs observed for any service).
- v4 audit: `docs/reference/v4-audit-2026-11-22.md`, Part A (Group A) and
  Part C (Group C).

## Method — this is a DELTA, not a fresh full audit

For each service, only findings in the NEW survey that differ from, add
to, or contradict the OLD survey and/or the v4 audit are listed. Items
where the new survey agrees with the old survey and the audit are not
re-litigated; the audit's verdicts stand for them unless the code
spot-check below shows the code has since changed.

Code spot-check: `frontend/src/pages/` read by grep only, no builds,
no edits. **Builds are in progress in this tree, so every code verdict
is "as of read time" (~22:22 IST, 2026-11-22) and may already be stale.**
Several v4-audit findings are already stale in code (IAM policy detail
tabs, VPC's 4 missing sections, WAF/KMS/CloudTrail/CloudWatch nav
expansion, Budgets Reports tab) — each is flagged where seen.

## Global deltas (apply to every service)

1. **The account is nearly empty.** Only resources: default VPC
   vpc-0612fead9ab13370f, 3 default subnets, 1 route table, 1 IGW,
   1 DHCP set, 1 NACL, 1 SG, 12 IAM roles. Consequence recorded in the
   new survey: **detail tabs could only be observed for VPC and IAM
   roles/policies; every other service's detail tabs are NOT OBSERVABLE
   (empty tables), never invented.** The old survey likewise observed
   no detail tabs for any service, so all detail tabs were SDR in the
   v4 audit. The new survey changes that for exactly two services
   (VPC, IAM) and confirms NOT OBSERVABLE for all the rest.
2. **Three wizards were opened for the first time** (all Cancel, nothing
   submitted): Create VPC, Launch instances, Lambda Create Event
   Function. The old survey opened no wizard, so every wizard step in
   the new survey is a NEW finding; all wizard steps were SDR in the
   v4 audit. No other service's wizard was observed.
3. **Services NOT reached in the new pass** (no findings claimed):
   Kinesis, MSK, Redshift, Neptune, EKS, CodePipeline/CodeBuild, Glue,
   Athena, EMR, SES, Global Accelerator, Route 53 Resolver endpoints,
   Cloud WAN, Network Firewall, Verified Access, PrivateLink endpoints,
   Lattice. Note: Cloud WAN / Network Firewall / Verified Access /
   PrivateLink endpoints / Lattice appear as VPC nav groups in the new
   survey's VPC section, but were not opened as standalone services.
4. **Nav regrouping is common.** Several services' left navs changed
   shape between the two surveys (EC2, Lambda, Route 53, WAF,
   CloudTrail, CloudWatch, Cost Explorer). Code that matched the OLD
   survey's nav may now be wrong against the NEW survey, and
   vice versa.

## 16. Cross-cutting deltas

1. **S3 IAM Access Analyzer column is the one direct
   survey-vs-survey contradiction in Group 1.** Old survey says
   the Buckets table has it; new survey's observed table does
   not. Code follows the old survey. Do not "fix" by deleting
   the column on the strength of one pass alone — the column may
   be conditional on an analyzer existing — but the discrepancy
   must be recorded and re-checked on a non-empty account.
2. **WAF is now a different console (wafv2-pro).** The code
   follows the old WAF console. Renames (Web ACLs →
   Protection packs), dropped items (Rules, Bot Control) and
   the 10 Protection-pack columns are the largest single
   nav/column delta in the group.
3. **Cost Explorer's observed breakdown changed shape**
   (Service/% → monthly columns). The code's 3/3 breakdown
   match from the v4 audit no longer holds.
4. **V4 work already in progress has overtaken the audit in
   at least 7 places** (read-time code, may change): IAM policy
   detail tabs (4/4 present); VPC Verified Access / Cloud WAN /
   Lattice / DNS Firewall sections (present as UNSUPPORTED);
   WAF/KMS/CloudTrail/CloudWatch full nav labels (present);
   Budgets Reports tab + "Find a budget" search (present);
   IAM editor Specify/Review flow, Import policy, MFA/IP
   conditions (present). The v4 audit's verdicts on those items
   should be treated as stale.
5. **Detail tabs remain unvalidated everywhere except VPC and
   IAM.** New survey: VPC 6 tabs observed; IAM role 5 tabs and
   IAM policy 4 tabs observed; everything else NOT OBSERVABLE.
   CloudForge's EC2 (9), S3 (5) and Lambda (6) detail tabs, and
   all stacked-card details, are still CloudForge's own design.
6. **Wizard steps are now observed for exactly 3 services**
   (VPC, EC2, Lambda). All other services' create flows remain
   SDR on wizard steps. The code's EC2 (5 steps) and Lambda
   (4 steps) wizards do not match the newly observed AWS
   wizards; VPC has no wizard at all in code.
7. **No pagination / Preferences gear delta.** The new survey
   does not add pagination or Preferences observations beyond
   what the old survey recorded, so the audit's cross-cutting
   finding (no pagination anywhere, Preferences disabled
   everywhere) stands unchanged for Group 1.

## 17. What was NOT checked

- Content depth of the new v4-in-progress code (e.g. whether
  the IAM policy detail tabs' Summary view matches AWS's
  "Allow (484 of 484 services)" table, whether the IAM editor's
  access-level groups / Linter exist, whether CloudTrail's

  an analyzer existing (needs a non-empty account to resolve).
- TGW Route tables / Policy tables, VPN tab set beyond the
  connections table, and the full VPC sub-item lists (only nav
  labels were checked).
- No builds, no tests, no live browser checks. No code was
  changed.

## Delta Part 2 — messaging/serverless/containers + remaining lite + restricted

# CloudForge v4 — survey-delta audit, Group 2
## Messaging / serverless / containers + remaining lite + restricted

Date: 2026-11-22 (written ~22:30 IST). Auditor: subagent (Group 2).

Scope (per task): SQS, SNS, EventBridge, API Gateway, ECS, ELB, DataSync,
Transit Gateway, VPN, CloudFront, EFS, GuardDuty, Security Hub, FSx, DMS,
Organizations, Direct Connect — plus services NOT reached in the new pass:
Kinesis, MSK, Redshift, Neptune, EKS, CodePipeline/CodeBuild, Glue, Athena,
EMR, SES, Global Accelerator, Route 53 Resolver endpoints, Cloud WAN,
Network Firewall, Verified Access, PrivateLink endpoints, Lattice.

Sources (read in full):
- NEW primary survey (authority for anything it records):
  `docs/reference/aws-console-survey-v4-2026-11-22.md`
  (fresh, 2026-11-22 21:57–22:20 IST, ap-southeast-2, read-only).
- OLD survey: `docs/reference/aws-console-survey-2026-11-22.md` (46/46).
- V4 audit already written: `docs/reference/v4-audit-2026-11-22.md`
  Parts B and D, plus `/tmp/v4-audit-part-b.md` and `/tmp/v4-audit-part-d.md`.

Method: DELTA only, not a fresh full audit. For each service, every finding
in the NEW survey that differs from, adds to, or contradicts the OLD survey
and the v4 audit. Column lists are copied VERBATIM from the new survey
where the new survey records them. Detail tabs are marked NOT OBSERVABLE
where the new survey says so — never invented.

Code spot-check: `frontend/src/pages/` ONLY (headers / subNav / table
`header:` values / section renders, via grep + targeted reads). No code
was edited. **Code state is "as of read time" — builds are in progress in
this tree, so it may already have changed.** Where the code differs from
what the v4 audit recorded, that is flagged `CODE CHANGED SINCE V4 AUDIT`
— the audit's verdict for that item is stale, not wrong at audit time.

Verdict key (code column): ✅ present in code as read · ⚠️ partial
(present but incomplete / different label / disabled / honest "Not
available") · ❌ missing in code as read. "New vs old" column: NEW =
first recorded in the new survey · SAME = identical in old survey ·
CHANGED = recorded in old survey but different in new · CONFIRMED = new
survey agrees with old survey (worth recording because it re-validates
the audit baseline).

## Headline deltas

1. **First-ever exact list columns** for SQS (7), SNS (3), EventBridge
   event buses (5), API Gateway (8), ECS clusters (6), Transit Gateway
   gateways (4), CloudFront (9, same as old minus checkbox),

3. **VPN columns grew 19 → 23**: the CIDR group is now 4 explicit

4. **DataSync is a pure CONFIRM**: new survey matches the old survey's

5. **GuardDuty / Security Hub: no delta.** Same restricted-dialog

These v4-audit statements were true at audit time and are no longer true
as read. All are code changes, not survey errors:

   (:195, :298) and Cloud WAN (:199, :306) — all as honest UNSUPPORTED
   sections with disabled Create buttons. Content is still absent, but
   the labels/sections now exist.
2. **VPN "4/5 nav items missing entirely" + "no VPN API exists" +
   registry contradiction (Part D §24, §35).** STALE. Real
   `CustomerGatewaysSection` (:1335) and `VpnGatewaysSection` (:1473)
   exist, wired to a real API-EMULATED backend; the VPN section's home
   card now says API-EMULATED (:268); the connections table renders
   real gateway IDs/addresses. The registry contradiction for VPN no
   longer holds as read. (EBS / Auto Scaling not re-checked here.)
3. **DataSync "nav matching 2, partial 3 (same-page sections)" +
   "Dashboard has no 'New' badge" (Part D §19).** STALE. All 5 nav
   items are now real sections with a "New" badge on Dashboard
   (MigrationConsoles.tsx:427–431).
4. **VPC VPN toolbar "no Create VPN connection button at all" (Part D
   §24).** PARTLY STALE. A create form now exists in the VPN section
   (name + customer-gateway + VPN-gateway selects). Search/pagination/
   Actions/Download configuration were not seen in the spot-check.

Not stale (re-confirmed as read): SQS/SNS/EventBridge/API Gateway/ECS
nav + columns (Part B §§1–5); ELB 11/11 columns, no toolbar (Part A §7);

These services are NOT in Group 2 (they belong to Groups A/C); their
column lists are reproduced here verbatim from the new survey only
because the task explicitly asked for them to be copied exactly. No
delta analysis is done for them here.

- **Cost Explorer / Budgets (Billing, global).** Cost Explorer breakdown
  columns: **Total, May–Oct 2026.** Budgets columns: **Name, Thresholds,
  Budget, Amount used, Forecasted amount, Current vs. budgeted,
  Forecasted vs. budgeted.** Left nav: Cost Management (Cost Explorer,
  Free Tier, Budgets).
- **CloudTrail.** Trails table: **Name, Status.** Event history:
  **Event name, Event time, Event source.** Banner: "CloudTrail Lake is
  no longer open to new customers starting May 31, 2026".
- **KMS.** Customer managed keys columns: **Aliases, Key ID, Status, Key
  type, Key spec, Key usage.**
- **Secrets Manager.** Secrets columns: **Secret name, Description, Last
  retrieved (UTC), Created on (UTC).** Left nav: only Secrets.
- **DynamoDB.** Tables columns: **Name, Status, Partition key, Sort key,
  Indexes, Replication Regions, Deletion protection, Favorite, Read
  capacity mode, Write capacity mode, Total size, Table class.**
- **ElastiCache.** Valkey caches columns: **Cache name, Status,
  Description, Engine version, Configuration, Created date.**

## Caveats

- Restricted-service nav items (FSx, DMS, Organizations, Direct
  Connect) were recorded behind paid-plan / advanced-features dialogs.
  They are nav labels, not verified page structures; building full
  sections from them would be building from labels, not from observed
  pages. The old survey's guidance for GuardDuty/Security Hub applies
  equally: use public console documentation as the reference and never
  present the restriction as the design.
- The new survey's "Domain name (standard)" (CloudFront) and the
  combined-vs-split CIDR headers (VPN) are labelling nuances, not new
  features; they are recorded as such above.
- CloudFront "Invalidations" and "What's new" appear in the old survey
  and in code but NOT in the new survey's nav list. That is recorded as
  "absent from new nav", not as a confirmed AWS removal — the new pass
  may simply not have recorded them.
- ELB's checkbox column: old survey listed it, new survey does not.
  Recorded as UNCLEAR, not as a confirmed removal.
- Code spot-check was grep + targeted reads of `frontend/src/pages/`
  only. No builds or tests were run; no code was changed.
